Because we were short-handed, my schedule got shifted into late-evening coverage, and I ended up taking on a lot heavier crisis paperwork. That's really when I started noticing that I just couldn't keep my focus.\n\nDoctor: Besides the difficulty concentrating and making decisions, how has your mood been over this past month? Have you been feeling sad, anxious, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Um... I haven't really noticed feeling sad, or even irritable, honestly. I guess I'm mostly just frustrated with myself because I can't seem to do my job right anymore. But as for my actual mood, I wouldn't say I've been feeling anxious or down. I haven't really noticed a big change there. It's just my focus that's completely shot.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep, appetite, or energy levels over this past month? And have you had any physical symptoms like headaches, dizziness, or feeling unwell?\n\nPatient: My sleep has definitely changed. Um, I get home from work really tired, but then I just lie in bed for hours, kind of replaying the day in my head. And if I do manage to fall asleep, I end up waking up before my alarm even goes off... and I feel like I barely slept at all. \n\nSo my energy is pretty low just from dealing with that. But as for my appetite, or any physical things like headaches or dizziness... no, I haven't really noticed any issues there. It's really just the sleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any recent head injuries, infections, or been evaluated by a primary care doctor or neurologist for any medical conditions that might explain these concentration problems?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't had any head injuries or... or any infections lately. And I haven't seen a regular doctor or a neurologist about this either. I haven't really been evaluated for anything medical, no.\n\nDoctor: When you're lying in bed replaying the day, are you experiencing worry or racing thoughts about work, or is it more just passive thinking without that anxious quality?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say it's racing thoughts, exactly. I'm not really lying there panicking or feeling super anxious about it. It's more just... I can't turn my brain off. I just keep going over the mistakes I made that day, or the things I couldn't focus on, and I just feel frustrated with myself. But it's not like a frantic worry, if that makes sense. It's just kind of a heavy, passive loop that keeps me awake.\n\nDoctor: During the day at work, not just at night in bed, do you find yourself experiencing sudden episodes of worry, panic, or physical symptoms like a racing heart or shortness of breath? Or is the difficulty concentrating happening without those kinds of anxiety symptoms?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really have anything like that. No sudden panic or... my heart doesn't race or anything. I don't feel short of breath. It's just the trouble focusing at work, but it's not because I'm feeling anxious or panicky during the day.\n\nDoctor: If your sleep were back to normal, do you think your concentration and focus at work would improve, or do you feel like the concentration problems would still be there even if you were sleeping well?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean, being exhausted all the time definitely doesn't help, but... I don't know if sleeping better would completely fix my focus. It feels like even if I did get a full night's rest, my brain would still just be kind of foggy. So, yeah, I really don't know if the concentration problems would go away just from sleeping well.\n\nDoctor: When you're trying to focus on your client's words or read the chart notes, are the thoughts that interrupt you unwanted or intrusive—like your mind is pulling you away against your will—or does it feel more like your mind just isn't working well, like the mental effort isn't there?\n\nPatient: It's definitely more that... my mind just isn't working well. It's not intrusive thoughts pulling me away, it's just that I can't seem to keep my head clear at work lately. Like, I'll be sitting there and I just completely lose track of what my clients are saying. Or I'll end up rereading the same chart note over and over again because the words just don't sink in. It feels like the mental effort just isn't there, and then I just kind of freeze on really simple decisions that I used to handle without any issue.\n\nDoctor: Over this past month, have you been feeling a persistent sense of worry or tension about things in general—not just frustration about work performance, but worry about your ability to handle things or concerns about what might happen—that's been present most days?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed any kind of constant worry or tension like that. It's really just the frustration with work and... you know, not being able to think clearly. But I don't sit around worrying about what might happen or feeling tense about life in general most days. I just feel more drained than anything else.\n\nDoctor: Before this month when your schedule changed, had you ever experienced periods where you felt like your thinking was foggy or your concentration was poor, even for a short time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Before this past month, my focus was pretty normal. I mean, sure, I'd get tired sometimes like anyone else, but I never had this kind of brain fog or trouble concentrating before the schedule change. It's really just a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Since your schedule changed a month ago, have you been feeling worried or anxious about things outside of just your work performance—like your health, finances, relationships, or other areas of your life—even if just occasionally?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, my health and finances and all that are fine. I haven't really been anxious about anything outside of work. It's... it's honestly just the work stuff and my brain feeling so foggy. I don't really sit around worrying about the rest of my life right now, I just feel too drained for that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
